- **Emboss.** seg15:0x7a62(char) reads the current position P and draws the one-byte string three times: 0x00FFFFFF at (P.x − 1, P.y − 1), PALETTERGB(0x60, 0x00, 0xC0) at (P.x + 1, P.y + 1), and 0x00FF00FF below 8 bpp (DGROUP:0x90ee < 8) or PALETTERGB(0xA0, 0x60, 0xFF) at P. It then does MoveTo(P.x + GetTextExtent(char).cx, P.y). The colour set at seg15:0x83e9 is always overwritten. [BYTES seg15:0x7a73 seg15:0x7a99 seg15:0x7ab6 seg15:0x7adc seg15:0x7aed seg15:0x7af3 seg15:0x7aff seg15:0x7b11 seg15:0x7b27]
- **Bolt to the cursor.** Each typing call with c ≠ −1 calls seg15:0x80b0(W/2, H/2 − 20, x, y) before it draws, with x = W/2 − Wt/2 + Wt · (c/2) / strlen and y = H/2 − 150 or H/2 + 100; the +10 at seg15:0x835c is dead because the two halvings agree. seg15:0x80b0 finishes any pending bolt (seg15:0x80da), stores (x, y) in DGROUP:0x3b98 and calls seg11:0xa4a6(W/2, H/2 − 20, x, y, 0xe). Full-line redraws draw no bolt. [BYTES seg15:0x830e seg15:0x8318 seg15:0x8344 seg15:0x834e seg15:0x835c seg15:0x837b seg15:0x80bb seg15:0x80c4 seg15:0x80ce]
- **Bolt shape.** seg11:0xa4a6(x1, y1, x2, y2, type) first calls srand(GetCurrentTime()) ([rng.md](rng.md)) and clears the branch counts DGROUP:0x9526 and DGROUP:0x9528. With dx = x2 − x1, dy = y2 − y1 and g = max(|dx| + |dy|, 3) it takes L = ((dx² + dy²)/g + g)/2 (one Newton step, seg8:0x1290). The segment length S is 30, or 50 when L/30 > 22; when L/50 > 22 it returns without drawing. N = L/S. The main points DGROUP:0x6b5c[i] are P0 = (x1, y1), P_N = (x2, y2) and, for 0 < i < N, x = x1 + ((rand%10 + (i+1)S − 5)·dx)/L + ((rand%26 − 13)·(−dy))/L and y = y1 + ((rand%10 + (i+1)S − 5)·dy)/L + ((rand%26 − 13)·dx)/L, with the rand calls in that order and each product divided separately. [BYTES seg11:0xa4bb seg11:0xa4c3 seg11:0xa4c9 seg11:0xa4d4 seg11:0xa4e2 seg11:0xa529 seg11:0xa537 seg11:0xa53c seg11:0xa54d seg11:0xa559 seg11:0xa7d5 seg11:0xa83a seg11:0xa884 seg11:0xa897 seg8:0x1290]
  - After each main point P_i (i = 0 … N − 1), rand()%3 = 0 adds branch b (DGROUP:0x6bbc + 20b, 5 points): point 0 = P_i and, for k = 1–4 with p = (32·(i%2) − 16)·k, x = P_i.x + ((rand%24 + p − 12)·(−dy))/L + ((rand%15 + 15k)·dx)/L, y = P_i.y + ((rand%24 + p − 12)·dx)/L + ((rand%15 + 15k)·dy)/L. More than 16 branches aborts the whole bolt with nothing drawn. Then rand()%5 ≤ 3 adds sub-branch s (DGROUP:0x6cfc + 12s, 3 points) from branch point 2 (Q): for m = 1, 2 with q = 12·(2(m+2)·(i%2) − m), x = Q.x + ((rand%16 + q − 32)·(−dy))/L + ((rand%15 + 15(m+2))·dx)/L, y = Q.y + ((rand%16 + q − 32)·dx)/L + ((rand%15 + 15(m+2))·dy)/L. [BYTES seg11:0xa561 seg11:0xa56e seg11:0xa585 seg11:0xa5c6 seg11:0xa614 seg11:0xa66c seg11:0xa67b seg11:0xa684 seg11:0xa696 seg11:0xa6ac seg11:0xa752 seg11:0xa7a9 seg11:0xa7b8]
  - It draws at once with the clipped line routine seg12:0x7256: the main polyline P0 … PN with CreatePen(PS_SOLID, 2, 0x00FFFFC3 = RGB(0xC3, 0xFF, 0xFF)), or 0x0000FF00 for type 9, and every branch (4 segments) and sub-branch (2 segments) with a 1-px pen of the same colour. It then sets DGROUP:0x1016 = N + 1. [BYTES seg11:0xa8a3 seg11:0xa8bc seg11:0xa8c1 seg11:0xa8ea seg11:0xa932 seg11:0xa968 seg11:0xa9ac seg11:0xa9de]
- **Bolt finish.** seg15:0x80da does nothing while DGROUP:0x1016 = 0. With Calibrate < 1400 it makes one seg11:0xa9ea(0) call and sets DGROUP:0x1016 = 0; otherwise it calls seg11:0xa9ea(0) until DGROUP:0x1016 = 0. seg11:0xa9ea sets OPAQUE. When DGROUP:0x1016 > 0 it redraws the main polyline with a black 2-px pen and the branches with a black 1-px pen; if DGROUP:0x1036 = 0 it then redraws only the branches and sub-branches again with stock BLACK_PEN and negates DGROUP:0x1016. When DGROUP:0x1016 < 0 it redraws everything with RGB(0x7F, 0xFF, 0xFF) pens (2 px main, 1 px branches), then sets DGROUP:0x1036 = 1 and negates if DGROUP:0x1036 was 0, else clears both. A positive value with DGROUP:0x1036 ≠ 0 draws black and clears both. From DGROUP:0x1036 = 0 on the port's path, one seg15:0x80da call erases the bolt, flashes it in RGB(0x7F, 0xFF, 0xFF) and erases it again, ending with DGROUP:0x1016 = DGROUP:0x1036 = 0. Each bolt stays visible until the next seg15:0x80da. [BYTES seg15:0x80e4 seg15:0x80eb seg15:0x80f5 seg15:0x80fb seg15:0x8105 seg15:0x810b seg11:0xa9fd seg11:0xaa02 seg11:0xaa65 seg11:0xaaf9 seg11:0xabb0 seg11:0xabbf seg11:0xac61 seg11:0xac6e seg11:0xac78]
- **Erase.** Once DGROUP:0x6e2c (name completion + 3000 ms) < now the entry is erased within one frame. k = 1 when MixChannels DGROUP:0x920a = 1, else 4. The top line gets k times: x = rand()%300 + W/2 − 150, seg15:0x80b0(W/2, H/2 − 20, x, H/2 − 150), sound seg10:0x754a(0x22, x, H/2 − 150, 4) and a 40 ms busy wait on GetCurrentTime; then seg15:0x80da, PatBlt(0, H/2 − 150, W, 40, BLACKNESS), the logo StretchBlt again and a 270 ms busy wait. The bottom line gets the same k bolts to (rand()%300 + W/2 − 150, H/2 + 100), then seg15:0x80da and PatBlt(0, H/2 + 100, W, 40, BLACKNESS). It then sets DGROUP:0x1e50 = now and n = DGROUP:0x95d8 + 1. For n ≤ 5, DGROUP:0x130c = now + 1000: one second of logo alone, then seg15:0x811a. For n > 5, DGROUP:0x6e28 = now + 4000 and DGROUP:0x6e2c = now + 9000, so after 4000 ms of logo alone the phase ends with the normal transition (sound 0x6e, black, phase 4). [BYTES seg15:0x7e7b seg15:0x7ea1 seg15:0x7ec4 seg15:0x7edf seg15:0x7eee seg15:0x7efb seg15:0x7f24 seg15:0x7f43 seg15:0x7f9e seg15:0x7fa8 seg15:0x7fcd seg15:0x7ffe seg15:0x8043 seg15:0x8062 seg15:0x806c seg15:0x8073 seg15:0x8077 seg15:0x807e seg15:0x8095 seg15:0x809b]
- **Timeline on the port's path.** 83 frames of logo growth come alone; the 84th clamps DGROUP:0x95d0 to 300 and already makes the first typing call (c = 0, a bolt and sound 0x14). Each entry then takes floor(L1/2) + 2 frames for the heading and floor(L2/2) + 2 for the name (the last frame of each is the completion redraw, with no bolt or sound), a 3000 ms hold, a blocking erase of about 40 + 270 + 40 ms plus drawing, and for entries 0–4 a 1000 ms logo-only gap followed by one reload frame that does not type. After entry 5 comes a 4000 ms logo-only tail. Each reload sets the deadline to now + 90000 ms, so that deadline never ends the phase. The Calibrate tests on this path are seg15:0x7cef and seg15:0x7d10 (< 800), seg15:0x7e3a (> 900, with MixChannels > 1 at seg15:0x7e42) and seg15:0x80eb (< 1400). [BYTES seg15:0x7d2c seg15:0x7da8 seg15:0x7e1c seg15:0x7efb seg15:0x7fa8 seg15:0x807e seg15:0x809b seg15:0x8169 seg15:0x7cef seg15:0x7d10 seg15:0x7e3a seg15:0x7e42 seg15:0x80eb] [DESIGN]
- **Skipped runs.** Because the entry resets neither DGROUP:0x95da, DGROUP:0x130c nor DGROUP:0x1c70 (written only at seg15:0x8276 and seg15:0x82fc, when c/2 = 0), a run skipped with Space in the middle of typing leaves the next run's first heading call with c/2 > 0: the heading skips the width measurement, is placed and aimed with the previous line's width, and appears partly typed, or completes at once when the left-over c/2 exceeds its length. A run skipped in the 1000 ms gap makes the next run reload entry 0 again on its first text frame, which costs one frame. [BYTES seg15:0x7b34 seg15:0x7e31 seg15:0x80a1 seg15:0x7dcb seg15:0x8243 seg15:0x8276 seg15:0x82fc seg15:0x833c seg15:0x83a5]

**Repaint.** A repaint in state 0 (seg11:0xafe case 0) always ends with seg15:0x5962(1). Before that, phase 0 changes nothing. Phases 1 and 2 set the deadline to now, so the repaint ends the black second or the title. Phases 3–5 move a deadline that is not already DGROUP:0x1e50 to now + 15000 ms. Phases 6 and 7 set DGROUP:0x92d4 = 9 when it is ≥ 2, else copy the desk bitmap DC DGROUP:0x929a to the screen. [BYTES seg11:0x15fc seg11:0x1603 seg11:0x160a seg11:0x162f seg11:0x1636 seg11:0x163b seg11:0x1642 seg11:0x164b seg11:0x1661 seg11:0x166a]

**Input.**
- The tick's state-0 case passes the pending message to seg11:0x7902 with the raw wParam. Only input that arrives during a frame goes through the router seg11:0x6aa8, which remaps key codes through seg11:0xb306. [BYTES seg7:0x21e seg7:0x22b seg2:0x77b seg2:0x79d seg11:0x6aff]
- No key reaches the state-0 handler before the first Calibrate measurement ends. Outside DEMO the input path needs DGROUP:0x1212 ≠ 0, and seg15:0x6009 sets DGROUP:0x1212 only when the measurement is accepted. Skipping the intro therefore never skips Calibrate, and seg6:0xa6c has called seg6:0xb3e (FrameRateSet) before any game starts. The input path also drops keys until DGROUP:0x3b94, which the dialog wrappers set to now + 500 ms after each dialog. [BYTES seg2:0x74a seg2:0x75c seg15:0x6009 seg15:0x6030 seg10:0x8401 seg10:0x8624]
- The state-0 input handler seg11:0x7902 acts only on WM_KEYUP (0x101). It first clears DGROUP:0x90f8 when it is set with DGROUP:0x8e5e = 0 (PLAY). It handles exactly the key codes below; every other key, Return included, does nothing. [BYTES seg11:0x7918 seg11:0x7b1b seg11:0x791e seg11:0x792c seg11:0x7935 seg11:0x793e seg11:0x7944 seg11:0x794a seg11:0x794e seg11:0x7952]

  | Key | Action | Evidence |
  |---|---|---|
  | S | sounds 0x6e and 0x13, New Demo through seg10:0x82a2(4); on a non-zero result deletes demogame.sav (seg6:0x92b8) and continues as D | [BYTES seg11:0x7959 seg11:0x7961 seg11:0x796b seg11:0x7975] |
  | D | seg10:0x38e0, then continues as C | [BYTES seg11:0x797a seg11:0x7981] |
  | C | stores seg10:0x804c(1) and seg10:0x804c(0) in DGROUP:0x939a and DGROUP:0x939e; when the phase is below 6 and DGROUP:0x120e = 0, sets the deadline to now, the phase to 5 and DGROUP:0x9516 to 1 for D or 0 otherwise. The next frame then takes the demo-start path, which restores demogame.sav only while DGROUP:0x9516 = 0 | [BYTES seg11:0x7990 seg11:0x799d seg11:0x79a4 seg11:0x79b3 seg11:0x79b7 seg11:0x79bd seg11:0x79c3 seg11:0x79cc] |
  | R | only while DGROUP:0x920c is 0 or 1: sound 0x51 and seg10:0x80a8, which opens the Order dialog (seg10:0x82a2(8)) at most 20 times per run (counter DGROUP:0x921e) and returns its result; a non-zero result runs seg10:0x885e(0) | [BYTES seg11:0x79d5 seg11:0x79dc seg11:0x79e5 seg11:0x79eb seg11:0x79f9 seg10:0x80b5 seg10:0x80bc seg10:0x80c7] |
  | ESC | the game-start flow ([Game-start dialogs](#game-start-dialogs)) | [BYTES seg11:0x7a02] |
  | Space | skips the current phase (deadline = now) when the phase is below 6 and DGROUP:0x120e = 0; otherwise, when DGROUP:0x92d4 = 1, calls seg15:0x1584(1) (desk-scene typing) | [BYTES seg11:0x7aea seg11:0x7af1 seg11:0x7af9 seg11:0x7afd seg11:0x7b03 seg11:0x7b0a seg11:0x7b13] |

  - Space during the title scene: on the next frame the title routine runs first and, since deadline − 200 < now, seg15:0x7962 sets DGROUP:0x94c4 = 1; the dispatcher then plays sound 0x6e, blacks the screen and enters the credits. [BYTES seg11:0x7aea seg15:0x7962 seg15:0x7840 seg15:0x59df]
  - seg10:0x80a8 returns its uninitialised local [bp−4] once the Order counter DGROUP:0x921e has reached 20, so a later 'R' can still run seg10:0x885e(0) at random. [BYTES seg10:0x80ba seg10:0x80d2 seg11:0x79f0] A port returns 0 there. [DESIGN]
- **Mouse.** A left click does nothing in state 0: WM_LBUTTONDOWN goes to seg16:0x638a, which returns unless DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x952c ≠ 0 (0 in the probe's title dump), and no mouse message reaches seg11:0x7902. A right click, once DGROUP:0x1212 ≠ 0, opens the popup menu ENG_LANG.DLL MENU 255 through seg2:0x134c, whose commands go to the WM_COMMAND handler. A double click goes to seg2:0x1046 when DGROUP:0x1212 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x22 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg16:0x6397 seg16:0x63aa seg2:0x683 seg2:0x68e seg2:0x1361 seg2:0x139b seg2:0x65e] [ORACLE probe-0001]

## Game-start dialogs
**Ordinal 10.** seg10:0x82a2(cmd) is the wrapper of ordinal 10 for every command except 0 and 3. seg10:0x7f18 issues command 0 (caller seg2:0x14ca) and seg10:0x8568 command 3 (callers seg2:0x55e, seg2:0xe5d, seg2:0x1500). INSPACE never issues commands 2, 10 and 13, and issues 9 only from the cmd-10 branch, which has no caller. Code −2 (before every ordinal-10 dialog of seg10:0x82a2 except command 10, before command 0 at seg10:0x7f31 and in seg10:0x8568) saves DGROUP:0x9220 into the byte DGROUP:0x7e0[DGROUP:0x73e] and sets DGROUP:0x9222 = −2. Every other code sets DGROUP:0x9222 = −3, and −3 is replaced by the saved byte (sign-extended) when that differs from DGROUP:0x9220. A resulting code ≥ 0 is stored in DGROUP:0x9220 (7 stored as 2), and a music deadline DGROUP:0x2384 below DGROUP:0x1e50 (unsigned) becomes DGROUP:0x1e50 + 9000. With DGROUP:0x9210 ≠ 0 it then calls ISPACE ordinal 7 command 11 with (code, arg), code being the value before the 7 → 2 mapping. seg10:0x82a2 issues −3 after every dialog except commands 10 and 12. **From ESC to the wave.** The path from the title to a wave runs as follows.

1. **ESC** plays sound 0x6e. In phase 6 it sets DGROUP:0x92d4 = 9 (the desk scene jumps to its last step). Otherwise it reads INI Accept. DGROUP:0x920c = 2 with Accept ≠ 42 opens Important Information (seg10:0x82a2(11)). It does nothing in state 1, while DGROUP:0x120e ≠ 0, while DGROUP:0x1212 = 0 and in phase 7. Below phase 6 it clears DGROUP:0x92e4, plays sound 0x13 and opens Restore Game through seg10:0x82a2(1). "&I Accept" (id 1) writes INI Accept = "42" and ends with 1; "&Cancel" (id 2) ends with 0. Neither button is the default. 8. **Monitor text.** The greeting is string 3032 formatted by seg10:0xa332 with (part ≠ 3, 591 + weekday, 639 + part, part == 3, 642, ThePlayer). weekday is tm_wday (0 = Sunday) from seg10:0x9764(1), and part comes from seg10:0x9764(0): 0 for hours 3–11, 1 for 12–17, 2 for 18–23 and 3 for 0–2. Part 2 is replaced by −41 before the call, so the evening reads 598 "Night" and 641 "Evening" is never used; hours 0–2 read 642 "Late". ## Window geometry
**Creation.** The main window's style, position and topmost flag at creation are under [Start-up](#start-up), step 15. [BYTES seg6:0x180e seg6:0x18a5 seg6:0x18d3]

**Show rectangle.** ShowLeft, ShowTop, ShowRight and ShowBottom live in DGROUP:0x1d20–0x1d26.
- When ShowRight ≤ 0 the start-up reader seg6:0x0 replaces the four Show values with a rectangle of 4/5 of the screen size, centred. It then moves a rectangle that reaches past the right screen edge left by the excess. One that reaches past the bottom loses twice the excess from ShowBottom while ShowTop stays, because seg6:0xfc and seg6:0x100 both add to DGROUP:0x1d26. [BYTES seg6:0x75 seg6:0x83 seg6:0x8a seg6:0xaf seg6:0xb1 seg6:0xc3 seg6:0xcd seg6:0xd7 seg6:0xe1 seg6:0xe7 seg6:0xeb seg6:0xf6 seg6:0xfc seg6:0x100]
- WM_MOVE (seg2:0x11c0) on a window that is not minimised stores GetWindowRect in the Show rectangle DGROUP:0x1d20 while Full = 0, and sets DGROUP:0x8f6a = 1 in both modes. seg6:0x33e writes the Show keys only while DGROUP:0x8f6a ≠ 0 and then clears it ([Exit](#exit)); the first WM_TIMER also clears it. [BYTES seg2:0x11ce seg2:0x11d7 seg2:0x11e5 seg2:0x11ea seg6:0x606 seg6:0x630 seg6:0x6a2 seg6:0x6be seg2:0x1211 seg2:0xb6e]

**Play area.** W = DGROUP:0x90ea is the play width, H = DGROUP:0x10 the client height, and DGROUP:0x90ec the play height above the HUD.
- **WM_SIZE.** The handler seg2:0xa74 ignores a minimised window. With Full (DGROUP:0x20) ≠ 0 and a window rectangle other than (0, 0, SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN) it calls MoveWindow(main, 0, 0, SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN, TRUE) and skips the size update. Otherwise it sets DGROUP:0x90ea = min(SM_CXSCREEN, cx), DGROUP:0x10 = min(SM_CYSCREEN, cy) (unsigned compares on the WM_SIZE client size) and DGROUP:0x90ec = DGROUP:0x10 − DGROUP:0xa70. [BYTES seg2:0xa85 seg2:0xab0 seg2:0xac5 seg2:0xaf5 seg2:0xafa seg2:0xb08 seg2:0xb15 seg2:0xb27 seg2:0xb34 seg2:0xb37 seg2:0xb3d]

It rebuilds the clip region through seg2:0xca4, copies DGROUP:0x8f78 to DGROUP:0x8f76 and sets DGROUP:0x8f64 = 1 when DGROUP:0x1e ≠ 0. With Full = 0 it sets DGROUP:0x8f6a = 1 and stores GetWindowRect(main) in DGROUP:0x1d20. With DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x90f4 = 1 it calls seg18:0x270e with DGROUP:0x10 and DGROUP:0x90ea. [BYTES seg2:0xafa seg2:0xb41 seg2:0xb4d seg2:0xb51 seg2:0xb57 seg2:0xb61 seg2:0xb6e seg2:0xb7c seg2:0xb81 seg2:0xb88 seg2:0xb97]
- seg2:0x1ac8 (state change) and seg2:0xba4 (re-layout) take the size from GetClientRect: width = client right, or SM_CXSCREEN when that is not greater; height likewise with SM_CYSCREEN. seg2:0x1ac8 sets DGROUP:0x90ec = height − DGROUP:0xa70 and DGROUP:0x8e5a = height − 85. It replaces the clip region DGROUP:0x12 with CreateRectRgn(0, 0, DGROUP:0x90ea, DGROUP:0x90ec), without the border inset of seg2:0xca4. seg2:0xba4 sets DGROUP:0xa70 = 85 and calls seg2:0xca4. It then blacks out the text-line rectangle DGROUP:0x1d28 (with Border) or the whole play area, and posts WM_PAINT. [BYTES seg2:0xc0c seg2:0xc33 seg2:0xc38 seg2:0xc3d seg2:0xc55 seg2:0xc86 seg2:0xc97]
- DGROUP:0xa70, the height of the HUD strip at the bottom of the client area, holds 85 in the file. Its only writer is seg2:0xbfc, which stores 85, and its readers are seg2:0xb3a and seg2:0x1b25. [BYTES seg2:0xbfc seg2:0xb3a seg2:0x1b25 DGROUP:0xa70]
- **Clip region.** seg2:0xca4 builds the play rectangle DGROUP:0x1d30 and the clip region DGROUP:0x12 = CreateRectRgn(L, T, W − L, DGROUP:0x90ec − m). PlayMode (DGROUP:0x9134) ≠ 0 adds 20 to m.   | Border (DGROUP:0xa78) | L | T | m | Text-line rectangle DGROUP:0x1d28..0x1d2e (x, y, cx, cy) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | ≠ 0 | 78 − (58 − W mod 58)/2 | 78 | 20 | (L, DGROUP:0x90ec − 40, W − 2L, 20) |
  | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | (0, DGROUP:0x10 − 105, W, 20) |

- DGROUP:0xa78 is INI Border (Game Border, default 1), stored by seg6:0xd51. When Border or Menu changes, the settings reader sets the menu, sets DGROUP:0x1038 = 1 and posts WM_PAINT; the next wave frame then calls the re-layout seg2:0xba4. seg11:0xafe draws the border as 58-pixel tiles with the same inset (58 − W mod 58)/2. [BYTES seg6:0xd20 seg6:0xd51 seg6:0xd86 seg6:0xd98 seg7:0x793 seg7:0x79a seg11:0xddd seg11:0xdef]
- **HUD.** seg11:0xafe lays out the wave HUD from the play size: the scrollbar (class "scrollbar", style 0x50000001, id 200) at (W/2 + 50, DGROUP:0x8e5a, 14, 85) when DGROUP:0x94e = 0, the IS_INFO panels id 210 at (DGROUP:0x93ba + 5, DGROUP:0x8e5a + 5, DGROUP:0x5024 − 9, 76) and id 220 at (DGROUP:0x93c4 + 5, DGROUP:0x8e5a + 5, DGROUP:0x5026 − 9, 76), and a PATCOPY fill of (0, DGROUP:0x8e5a, W, 85). The WM_PAINT routine seg2:0x970 calls seg11:0xafe while DGROUP:0x8f64 is set, which WM_SIZE sets, so a resize re-lays the HUD out at the new width and height. [BYTES seg11:0xca2 seg11:0xcb1 seg11:0xcbe seg11:0xcc2 seg11:0xcd9 seg11:0xd28 seg11:0xd36 seg11:0xd4e seg11:0xd79 seg11:0xd9f seg11:0xdcc seg2:0x9a9 seg2:0x9b0 seg2:0xb61]

When it finds DGROUP:0x8f64 = 0 it only sets it, without the HUD layout, so the HUD is laid out again from the next paint on. The writes that clear it are seg9:0x6649, seg9:0x6975, seg10:0x844a, seg10:0xa0c6 and seg11:0x3e5f. [BYTES seg2:0x98f seg2:0x9a3 seg2:0x9a9 seg2:0x9b7]

**Scale.**
- The visible world follows the client rectangle and the scale level. DGROUP:0x9150 holds 4 − Size, and the view extent in world units is W << (4 − Size) by DGROUP:0x90ec << (4 − Size). One pixel is 16, 8 or 4 world units at Size 0, 1 or 2. Nothing letterboxes or stretches the play area when the client size changes. [BYTES seg6:0xb07 seg9:0x2408 seg10:0x9b8d seg8:0x14 seg8:0x1b seg8:0x24 seg4:0x27d8 seg4:0x27e2 seg4:0x27f6]
- The scale level DGROUP:0x9156 (INI Size, default 1) takes the values 0, 1 and 2: seg10:0x9a2a beeps and changes nothing when Size + delta falls outside 0–2. The manual's 1/2x, 1x and 2x are levels 0, 1 and 2. [BYTES seg10:0x9a5f seg10:0x9a63 seg10:0x9a6a seg10:0x9a6e seg6:0xaf8]
- The writers of DGROUP:0x9156 are exactly seg6:0xafd, seg6:0x95ca, seg9:0x23bd, seg9:0x23fe, seg10:0x9ade, seg10:0x9b85, seg15:0x21c5, seg15:0x21d8, seg15:0x2bdc, seg15:0x2bf9, seg18:0x131, seg18:0x72d, seg18:0x800, seg18:0x903, seg18:0x910, seg18:0x2753 (shl by 1) and seg18:0x2ac3. [BYTES seg6:0xafd seg6:0x95ca seg9:0x23bd seg9:0x23fe seg10:0x9ade seg10:0x9b85 seg15:0x21c5 seg15:0x21d8 seg15:0x2bdc seg15:0x2bf9 seg18:0x131 seg18:0x72d seg18:0x800 seg18:0x903 seg18:0x910 seg18:0x2753 seg18:0x2ac3]
  - DGROUP:0x9150 is written only at seg6:0xb07, seg9:0x2408 and seg10:0x9b8d. The Size writes at seg6:0x95ca (saved-game header +0x171, which then sets DGROUP:0x92e2 and writes the INI), seg15:0x21c5/0x2bdc (temporarily 1) and in seg18 leave the view shift unchanged until one of those three runs. [BYTES seg6:0x95ca seg6:0x95ed seg15:0x21c5 seg15:0x21d8 seg18:0x2753 seg18:0x2ac3]
- **Rescale.** seg10:0x9a2a(delta) rescales only in state 1 with DGROUP:0x9130 = 0. Otherwise it sets DGROUP:0x103c = 1, and the wave frame later calls seg10:0x7f88, which rereads INI Size. While rescaling it shows ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 101 modeless through CreateDialog with procedure seg9:0x90fe and DGROUP:0x9518 = 1, so the procedure's WM_INITDIALOG puts string 2269 into control 114 and centres the dialog on the screen. At the end it destroys the dialog and posts 0x978. [BYTES seg10:0x9a39 seg10:0x9a47 seg7:0xb73 seg7:0xb7a seg10:0x9a7d seg10:0x9a95 seg9:0x9130 seg9:0x913c seg9:0x914b seg9:0x9196 seg10:0xa0d0 seg10:0xa0ea] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 101] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 142] id 2269
  - With Size set temporarily to delta + 1, the rescale rebuilds through seg10:0x6308 the images in slots 0–0x39 except 0x20. When DGROUP:0x103a is set (new Size 1 reached by +1, or by −1 with DGROUP:0x8fe8 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x90f0 = 0) it rebuilds only slots 14 to DGROUP:0x92ec + 13. It also rebuilds every non-zero image in DGROUP:0x4e94[0..DGROUP:0x9408). It then stores Size + delta and 4 − Size, and only afterwards doubles or halves the size words +0x22 and +0x24 of the 0x70-byte object records (shl by delta + 1, then sar 1) and reloads the ships. At the end it writes INI Size (and SetSize when DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0), destroys the dialog and blacks out the play area. Size scales sprites, object extents and the view together. [BYTES seg10:0x9ade seg10:0x9aa7 seg10:0x9b20 seg10:0x9b39 seg10:0x9b6d seg10:0x9b85 seg10:0x9b8d seg10:0x9c07 seg10:0x9c1b seg10:0x9de4 seg10:0xa09d seg10:0xa0ba seg10:0xa103 seg10:0x641f seg10:0x654f seg10:0x6584]
- **Keys.** Ctrl (GetKeyState VK_CONTROL) with VK_ADD or VK_OEM_PLUS calls seg10:0x9a2a(1), and with VK_SUBTRACT or VK_OEM_MINUS calls seg10:0x9a2a(−1); Alt with the same keys calls seg10:0x7e1c(±1) instead ([timing.md](timing.md#speed-keys)). seg10:0x7f88 rereads INI Size and steps seg10:0x9a2a by ±1, twice for a difference of 2, when DGROUP:0x3a38 ≠ 0; otherwise it sets DGROUP:0x92e2 and DGROUP:0x103c. [BYTES seg11:0x6cde seg11:0x6ce8 seg11:0x6cfd seg11:0x6d07 seg11:0x6e45 seg11:0x6ed4 seg11:0x6eda seg11:0x6ee0 seg11:0x71f7 seg11:0x7207 seg11:0x7260 seg10:0x7fa9 seg10:0x7fb8 seg10:0x7ff4 seg10:0x8008 seg10:0x800f seg10:0x803f]
  - A Ctrl+Plus/Minus pressed outside state 1 (or while DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0) is lost: seg10:0x9a2a only sets DGROUP:0x103c, and seg10:0x7f88 then compares INI Size with DGROUP:0x9156, which are unchanged, so it does nothing. While DGROUP:0x3a38 = 0, seg10:0x7f88 sets DGROUP:0x103c again on every frame. [BYTES seg10:0x9a47 seg7:0xb7a seg10:0x7fb1 seg10:0x7fb4 seg10:0x803f]
  - Plain Plus or Minus (no Ctrl or Alt) moves the HUD scrollbar: DGROUP:0xa6c changes by ±8, clamped at 500 above, SetScrollPos(DGROUP:0xa7a, SB_CTL, 600 − value), then seg11:0x33cc runs. [BYTES seg11:0x720f seg11:0x7226 seg11:0x7233 seg11:0x7242 seg11:0x7247 seg11:0x7262]

**Mode switching.**
- **System commands.** seg2:0xd78 dispatches on wParam & 0xFFF0. Every unlisted code returns 1 and goes to DefWindowProc. [BYTES seg2:0xd85 seg2:0xd88 seg2:0xd90 seg2:0xd97 seg2:0xd9e seg2:0xda5 seg2:0xdac seg2:0xdb1 seg2:0xdb5 seg2:0xe6b seg2:0xe71 seg2:0xe74 seg2:0xe82 seg2:0xfa5 seg2:0xfb5 seg2:0xfba seg2:0x63f]

  | Code | Action | Returns |
  |---|---|---|
  | 0x60 "&Main Menus" | toggles the menu bar | whatever AX DrawMenuBar leaves |
  | 0xc0 | posts WM_COMMAND 0xc0 | 0 |
  | SC_MINIMIZE | pauses through seg2:0x9c4 | 1, so DefWindowProc minimizes |
  | SC_MAXIMIZE | seg2:0xfc4 when DGROUP:0x1e is set | 1, so DefWindowProc also maximizes |
  | SC_RESTORE | restores the windowed rectangle (below) | 1, so DefWindowProc also restores |
  | SC_CLOSE | the exit sequence ([Exit](#exit)) | 0 |
  | any other | none | 1 |

- **SC_RESTORE** sets DGROUP:0x8f68 = 1 and moves the window to the ShowLeft–ShowBottom rectangle. The first time (DGROUP:0x14) it appends a separator and "&Main Menus" (string 2184, id 0x60, checked when Menu is non-zero) to the system menu. It returns to full screen through seg2:0xfc4 when Full is non-zero, and sets the caption to string 2337 while DGROUP:0x8f66 is set. [BYTES seg2:0xe87 seg2:0xe8b seg2:0xea6 seg2:0xeaa seg2:0xeaf seg2:0xebc seg2:0xec1 seg2:0xece seg2:0xed7 seg2:0xef3 seg2:0xf07 seg2:0xf15 seg2:0xf1d seg2:0xf20 seg2:0xf45] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 137] id 2184 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 147] id 2337
- **Command 0x60** flips INI Menu (DGROUP:0x90f6) between 0 and 1, attaches DGROUP:0x90fa or no menu with SetWindowWord(main, GWW_ID, …), checks or unchecks item 0x60 and calls DrawMenuBar. [BYTES seg2:0xf4d seg2:0xf5b seg2:0xf64 seg2:0xf75 seg2:0xf8b seg2:0xf97 seg2:0xfa0]
- **Full screen.** seg2:0xfc4 sets DGROUP:0x20 = 1, ANDs the style with 0xFF30FFFF (clearing WS_CAPTION, WS_SYSMENU, WS_THICKFRAME and both boxes) and calls MoveWindow(main, 0, 0, SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN, TRUE). It makes the window HWND_TOPMOST when OnTop is non-zero. [BYTES seg2:0xfd1 seg2:0xfdd seg2:0xfeb seg2:0xff3 seg2:0x1012 seg2:0x101d seg2:0x1039]
- **Double click.** A double click outside the three HUD hotspots toggles the mode once DGROUP:0x1e is set. From full screen it ORs 0x00CF0000 into the style, calls SetWindowPos(main, HWND_NOTOPMOST, 0, 0, 0, 0, SWP_NOSIZE\|SWP_NOMOVE), posts SC_RESTORE and sets DGROUP:0x20 = 0; the SC_RESTORE handler then moves the window to the Show rectangle. From windowed mode it calls seg2:0xfc4. WndProc reaches this only when DGROUP:0x1212 and DGROUP:0x22 are set. [BYTES seg2:0x1046 seg2:0x1152 seg2:0x1159 seg2:0x1177 seg2:0x117f seg2:0x1188 seg2:0x1194 seg2:0x119d seg2:0x11a7 seg2:0x11ac seg2:0x11b5 seg2:0x65e-0x66a]
- **Boss key.** WM_COMMAND 0x19c (Alt+B) ORs 0x00CF0000 into the style only while Full is non-zero and leaves DGROUP:0x20 at 1, then posts SC_MINIMIZE, which pauses and minimizes. The later SC_RESTORE therefore first moves the window to the Show rectangle and then goes back to full screen through seg2:0xfc4. [BYTES seg2:0x1486 seg2:0x14a4 seg2:0x14ac seg2:0x14b5 seg2:0xe70 seg2:0xe8b seg2:0xf15 seg2:0xf1d]
- **Full and DGROUP:0x22.** DGROUP:0x20 (Full) is written only at seg6:0x1a (INI), seg2:0xfd1 (1) and seg2:0x11ac (0). DGROUP:0x22 starts as 1 in the initialised data. It is written only at seg7:0x19 (1 on each tick pass while DGROUP:0x1a is 0) and at seg7:0xff, seg7:0x197, seg7:0x1bf and seg7:0x211 (0 in states 6, 8 and 9 and in state 0 while DGROUP:0x9294 is set). Its only readers are seg2:0x3eb (WM_GETMINMAXINFO) and seg2:0x665 (WM_LBUTTONDBLCLK), so in those states the window keeps its size and a double click cannot switch modes. [BYTES seg6:0x1a seg2:0xfd1 seg2:0x11ac DGROUP:0x22 seg7:0x10 seg7:0x19 seg7:0xff seg7:0x197 seg7:0x1bf seg7:0x20a seg7:0x211 seg2:0x3eb seg2:0x665]

**Watch windows.** IconWnd (DGROUP:0x8f72) and SmWnd (DGROUP:0x8f74) record whether the IS_ICON window (procedure seg7:0x6184) and the IS_SMART window (seg13:0x613a) are shown; their SC_CLOSE clears the key. VScale (DGROUP:0xb6e) and SScale (DGROUP:0x95f8) are the checks of each window's system-menu item 0xF0 "1/2x Scale". The window's WM_GETMINMAXINFO fixes its width to 50 >> scale and its height to SM_CYCAPTION + n·46 + 6 (n·23 + 3 at scale 1), with n = DGROUP:0x91fe or DGROUP:0x95fa. None of the four changes the main play area. [BYTES seg2:0x4f7 seg2:0x505 seg2:0x51a seg2:0x528 seg7:0x61e9 seg7:0x6233 seg7:0x627f seg7:0x62d5 seg7:0x62e1 seg7:0x62fd seg7:0x631c seg13:0x61ea seg13:0x6237 seg13:0x628d seg13:0x62b5 seg13:0x62d4 seg6:0x156c seg6:0x15c5]

**Colour depth.** The title screen draws "For best results, change Windows to 256 color mode" unless DGROUP:0x90ee is exactly 8 (step 13 of [Start-up](#start-up)). [BYTES seg15:0x71e8 seg15:0x721d]

## Palette
ISPACE.DLL owns the game palette; INSPACE drives it through ordinal 4 and ordinal 14 ([ispace.md](ispace.md)).

- **Ordinal 4** (ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x34c8, `retf 0xe`) reads hPal [bp+0x12], hwnd [bp+0x10], hdc [bp+0xe], a [bp+0xc], b [bp+0xa], c [bp+8] and cmd [bp+6] and calls Pascal routines, except command 8, which is cdecl. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x34eb seg5:0x34f5 seg5:0x34ff seg5:0x350e seg5:0x3520 seg5:0x352f seg5:0x3541 seg5:0x3550 seg5:0x355c seg5:0x3566 seg5:0x3592 seg5:0x39f1 seg5:0x446a seg5:0x4748]

  | Command | Routine | Purpose | INSPACE wrapper |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | seg5:0x3596(a) | create | seg10:0x6e00, a = 0 (callers seg2:0x469, seg6:0x1925) |
  | 1 | seg5:0x44ac(hPal, c, b) | load scheme c in mode b | seg10:0x6e76(c, b), then command 3 (16 callers) |
  | 2 | seg5:0x3aae(hPal) | delete | seg10:0x6e26 (seg2:0x458, seg6:0x1a53) |
  | 3 | seg5:0x4378(hPal, hwnd, hdc) | animate | seg10:0x6e4c, with the main window and DGROUP:0x13dc (8 callers) |
  | 4 | seg5:0x484a(hPal, hdc, c, b) | save to set b | seg10:0x6da6(b, c), then 3 (5 callers) |
  | 5 | seg5:0x474c(hPal, hdc, b) | restore set b | seg10:0x6dd4(b), then 3 (6 callers) |
  | 6 | seg5:0x3dee(hPal, a, b, c) | allocate a colour | seg10:0x6d7a(r, g, b) (3 callers, grey levels) |
  | 7 | seg5:0x446e(hPal, c) | allocate ramp c | seg10:0x6ea0(c) (seg10:0x56da with 9, seg15:0x6cfb with 5) |
  | 8 | seg5:0x3bae(hPal, c) | recolour black entries | none |

  [BYTES seg10:0x6e0a seg10:0x6e1c seg10:0x6e3e seg10:0x6e56 seg10:0x6e68 seg10:0x6e8a seg10:0x6e8e seg10:0x6e95 seg10:0x6db0 seg10:0x6dc2 seg10:0x6dc9 seg10:0x6dea seg10:0x6def seg10:0x6df6 seg10:0x6d8f seg10:0x6d98 seg10:0x6eb6 seg10:0x6eb9 seg10:0x9944 seg10:0x9956] seg10:0x993a(mode, percent) wraps ordinal 14 (8 callers in seg15).
- **Slots.** ISPACE.DLL keeps up to three palettes in parallel slot tables: handle DGROUP:0x1cd6[3], kind DGROUP:0x1e92, working LOGPALETTE far pointer DGROUP:0x116c, stored sets DGROUP:0x14a4[slot·2+k], mode DGROUP:0x1e9c. Every command finds the slot by handle and does nothing when the handle is not found. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x35b4 seg5:0x35d4 seg5:0x360d seg5:0x39b8 seg5:0x44f3 seg5:0x4396 seg5:0x43ae]
- **Command 0** allocates a 256-entry LOGPALETTE (GlobalAlloc(0x42, 1032), palVersion 0x300) and stores a as the palette's kind in ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1e92. It reads SIZEPALETTE (0 becomes 16), NUMRESERVED (0 becomes 16) and max(BITSPIXEL, PLANES) into ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1e9a. When that is 4 it copies system entries 0–15. Otherwise it copies system entries 0 … NUMRESERVED/2 − 1 into entries 0 …, and system entries SIZEPALETTE − NUMRESERVED/2 … into entries 256 − NUMRESERVED/2 …, the destination counted from the 256 entries. That gives (0, 10) and (246, 10) at 8 bpp and (0, 10) and (6, 10) at true colour, both landing in entries 0–9 and 246–255, which confirms the depth8-0002 reading. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x35a5 seg5:0x35d4 seg5:0x35e7 seg5:0x3619 seg5:0x3625 seg5:0x3639 seg5:0x3649 seg5:0x3651 seg5:0x367b seg5:0x3685 seg5:0x3691 seg5:0x3696 seg5:0x36a0 seg5:0x36c2 seg5:0x36dc seg5:0x36e6 seg5:0x36f2 seg5:0x3702] [ORACLE depth8-0001] [ORACLE depth8-0002]
  - GetSystemPaletteEntries writes straight into the LOGPALETTE entries, so they keep the peFlags GDI returns. On a 256-colour Windows display these are the default static colours: 0 000000, 1 800000, 2 008000, 3 808000, 4 000080, 5 800080, 6 008080, 7 c0c0c0, 8 c0dcc0, 9 a6caf0, 246 fffbf0, 247 a0a0a4, 248 808080, 249 ff0000, 250 00ff00, 251 ffff00, 252 0000ff, 253 ff00ff, 254 00ffff, 255 ffffff. In depth8-0002 both calls return 10, but the trace does not log the buffers. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x36e1 seg5:0x3707] [REF Windows SDK, default palette static colours] [REF Wine dlls/winex11.drv/palette.c static colour template] [ORACLE depth8-0002]
  - Unless max(BITSPIXEL, PLANES) is 4, command 0 sets entries 10–245 to black with peFlags PC_RESERVED; the count 236 is the constant 256 − 20, not derived from NUMRESERVED. It then calls CreatePalette, keeps the handle in ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1cd6, makes two stored copies of the LOGPALETTE (ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x14a4), fills the built-in colour tables through seg5:0x5846 and returns the handle. INSPACE keeps it in DGROUP:0x920e. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x3880 seg5:0x389e seg5:0x38bf seg5:0x38ca seg5:0x38e0 seg5:0x38ea seg5:0x3984 seg5:0x39b8 seg5:0x39dd seg5:0x39e5] [BYTES seg10:0x6e1c]
  - Command 0 also stores the first argument in ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1ac when hwnd ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x1ac = −1. INSPACE passes hwnd 0, so this never happens from INSPACE. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x34d8 seg5:0x34e8] [BYTES seg10:0x6e0a]
- **Colour tables.** seg5:0x5846 (only caller seg5:0x39dd, in command 0) fills ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x117c (18 rows of 10 RGB triples, 30 bytes per row, ending at DGROUP:0x1397) and DGROUP:0x1cdc (18 rows of 5 triples, 15 bytes per row, ending at DGROUP:0x1de9) with immediate byte stores and two interpolation loops; it reads no resource and calls nothing. The values are in [palette-tables.md](palette-tables.md), and they equal the probe dumps at the title, Navigation and wave. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x5846 seg5:0x39dd seg5:0x5854 seg5:0x61a7] [ORACLE probe-0001]
  - Loop 1 (seg5:0x597c–0x59b1) sets triple k = 1–8 of each 0x117c row, per channel, to T0 − idiv((T0 − T9)·k, 9) from the row's triples 0 and 9 (signed 16-bit, truncating). Loop 2 (seg5:0x59e3–0x5a19) sets triple k = 1–3 of each 0x1cdc row to U0 − trunc((U0 − U4)·k/4) (cwd / and dx, 3 / sar 2). Both run once over all 18 rows between the constant blocks seg5:0x5854–0x5967 and seg5:0x5a3b–0x61a7. The later block rewrites the whole middle of every row except 0x117c rows 0, 1, 3, 6, 10 and 12 and 0x1cdc rows 0, 1, 3, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12 and 13, which keep their interpolated middles. Row 12 of both tables is all zero after the call. Command 0 runs twice at start-up and the second call writes the same bytes. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x597c seg5:0x599c seg5:0x59ad seg5:0x59e3 seg5:0x59fe seg5:0x5a03 seg5:0x5a15] [ORACLE depth8-0002]
- **Command 3** is the only AnimatePalette in either module: AnimatePalette(hPal, 10, 236, &entries[10]) from the working LOGPALETTE, with the palette selected but not realised into hdc, or into GetDC(hwnd) when hdc is 0. Neither INSPACE.EXE nor ISPACE.DLL imports SetPaletteEntries, GetPaletteEntries, GetNearestPaletteIndex, ResizePalette or SetSystemPaletteUse. So after CreatePalette the game palette changes only through AnimatePalette of entries 10–245. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x43b3 seg5:0x43c6 seg5:0x43e3 seg5:0x4403 seg5:0x440f seg5:0x441a] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL relocation records] [BYTES INSPACE.EXE relocation records]
- **Command 1** (scheme c, mode b) records b. At 8 bpp and above it sets entries 10–245 to white for c = −1000, to black for c ≥ 0, and entries 10–182 to black for other negative c. For a kind-0 palette and c ≠ −1000 it copies the 92 triples of ISPACE.DLL #5500 100 into entries 11–102. c ≥ 100 or c = −2 then blacks entries 73–182 and subtracts 100. c > 4 allocates colour ramp c through seg5:0x61b2, and mode 0 also ramps 5–13. It calls no AnimatePalette itself; INSPACE's wrapper seg10:0x6e76 follows it with command 3. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x44f3 seg5:0x44f7 seg5:0x45c6 seg5:0x45d0 seg5:0x45f8 seg5:0x4632 seg5:0x4656 seg5:0x4667 seg5:0x4675 seg5:0x467d seg5:0x4688 seg5:0x4694 seg5:0x46cd seg5:0x46d4 seg5:0x46fc seg5:0x470d seg5:0x4731] [BYTES seg10:0x6e95]
  - ISPACE.DLL #5500 100 is 288 bytes: 92 RGB triples for entries 11–102, none of them black, then 12 zero bytes. Its first entries are 11 00ffff, 12 00e9ff, 13 00d3ff, 14 00bdff and its last 99 007200, 100 005e00, 101 005000, 102 003f00. Command 1 presets ramp records 0–4 over its groups: record 0 = 11–27 blue (00ffff … 000065), record 1 = 28–62 yellow-orange-brown (fff000 … 4b1200), record 2 = 73–89 grey (ffffff … 3f3f3f, step 12), record 3 = 63–72 khaki (9f9777 … 3b372a), record 4 = 90–102 green (c0ff00 … 003f00). It also presets records 5–18 to entries 103 + 10(r − 5) … 112 + 10(r − 5). seg5:0x2626 writes only bytes +4, +5 and +6 of each entry, so entries 11–102 keep the peFlags PC_RESERVED that command 0 set. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL 5500 100] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x263a seg5:0x2692 seg5:0x457d seg5:0x45b1 seg5:0x4521 seg5:0x4550 seg5:0x38bf] [ORACLE probe-0001]
  - Ramp positions: after command 1 with c ≥ 0 entry 10 is black but 11–102 are not, so the first black pair is 103/104. Mode 2 (the 0x9cf handler, seg2:0x484) and mode 1 (the title entry, seg15:0x5c35) put scheme 8 (row 7) at 103–112, and the title's command 7 with c = 5 (seg15:0x6cfb) then puts row 1 at 113–122. Mode 0 (seg10:0x37 and the other seg10:0x6e76(8, 0) callers) allocates 8 at 103–112, 5 at 113–122, 6 at 123–132, 7 at 133–142, 9 at 143–152, 10 at 153–162, 11 at 163–172, 12 at 173–182 and 13 at 183–192, leaving 193–245 black. The probe's ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1dea map agrees. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x44c8 seg5:0x461b seg5:0x4660 seg5:0x467d seg5:0x470d seg5:0x4719 seg5:0x4731 DGROUP:0x1dea DGROUP:0x1398] [BYTES seg2:0x484 seg15:0x5c35 seg15:0x6cfb seg10:0x37] [ORACLE probe-0001]
  - ISPACE.DLL #5500 100 is in RGB order: seg5:0x2626 copies byte 0 of each triple to peRed, byte 1 to peGreen and byte 2 to peBlue of entries 11–102, after seg5:0x254c loads the resource with FindResource(ISPACE.DLL, 100, 5500) ([formats.md](formats.md#ispacedll-5500-100)). [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x2566 seg5:0x256c seg5:0x263a seg5:0x265e seg5:0x2674 seg5:0x2688 seg5:0x2692] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL 5500 100]
- **Commands 4 and 5.** At 8 bpp and above, command 4 copies the RGB of working entries 10–245 into stored set b, blacks the working entries when c ≠ 0, and animates through command 3. Command 5 copies stored set b back into working entries 10–245 and animates. Neither touches peFlags. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x4859 seg5:0x48ad seg5:0x48c4 seg5:0x491f seg5:0x4922 seg5:0x4940 seg5:0x4963 seg5:0x475b seg5:0x47af seg5:0x47c6 seg5:0x481b seg5:0x483c]
- **Command 6** allocates a colour. It scans entries 10–244 upward for a kind-0 palette (245 downward otherwise). At each entry it returns −1 if R, G and B are each within 3 of (a, b, c), or writes (a, b, c) into the entry and returns its index if the entry is black or (1, 11, 111) and is not the first entry scanned. It returns −1 when the scan ends. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x3e0d seg5:0x3e6d seg5:0x3e95 seg5:0x3ee7 seg5:0x3f1c seg5:0x3ff2 seg5:0x4014 seg5:0x4065 seg5:0x406f seg5:0x4245 seg5:0x4253 seg5:0x4284]
- **Command 7** (seg5:0x446e → seg5:0x61b2(slot, c)) does nothing for c = −1 or a kind-1 palette and subtracts 100 when c ≥ 100. Unless the stored mode DGROUP:0x1e9c is 2, a ramp already recorded in DGROUP:0x1dea[mode·25 + c] is reused (its count DGROUP:0x1eb8 is incremented). Command 1 with mode ≠ 2 clears that table, and mode 2 reallocates every time. Otherwise it takes a free ramp record from 5–18 and finds the first two consecutive black working entries in 10–235. It copies ten RGB triples from ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x117c row r into them, r chosen by a switch on c (3→15, 5→1, 6→3, 7→5, 8→7, 9→17, 10→13, 11→14, 12→6, 13→2; c = 4 leaves r unset), and animates through command 3. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x61c1 seg5:0x61cc seg5:0x61ee seg5:0x6208 seg5:0x621a seg5:0x4504 seg5:0x6292 seg5:0x6314 seg5:0x643e seg5:0x6492]
  - The scan runs i = 10–235 and tests only bytes 0–2 (R, G, B) of entries i and i + 1, not peFlags. The copy writes bytes 0–2 of entries i … i + 9 whatever they hold, and the record stores i·4 and (i + 9)·4 at DGROUP:0x1398 + 4·(mode·19 + record). DGROUP:0x1dea[mode·25 + c] and DGROUP:0x1eb8 are marked before the scan. When no pair is found it animates without writing; when no free record exists (record 19) it exits without scanning. The values of c = 4 and of c outside 3–13 jump to seg5:0x62ad with the row unset. The ordinal 12 helper seg7:0x4ee8 has its own switch (seg7:0x4fc8) with the same map plus c 0 → row 8, 1 → 4, 2 → 0 and 4 → 10, and reads both tables ([palette-tables.md](palette-tables.md)). [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x62ad seg5:0x633d seg5:0x63e7 seg5:0x6410 seg5:0x641d seg5:0x6428 seg5:0x646a seg5:0x649e seg5:0x64d6 seg5:0x64e3 seg7:0x4fc8 seg7:0x505d seg7:0x53f0]
- **Command 8** works only on a kind-1 palette at exactly 8 bpp: c = 0 recolours black working entries 10–244 to (1, 11, 111) and c ≠ 0 turns (1, 11, 111) back to black. INSPACE creates its palette with kind 0 and none of its wrappers issues command 8. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x3bfe seg5:0x3c05 seg5:0x3ca4 seg5:0x3cbf seg5:0x3cda seg5:0x3d0e seg5:0x3d65] [BYTES seg10:0x6e0a seg10:0x6e18]
- **Ordinal 14** on a kind-0 palette scales the RGB bytes of a range of working entries by percent/100 in place, with the 16-bit arithmetic below. Mode 3 covers entries 185–229 (bytes from ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x178 = 0x2e4 to DGROUP:0x180 = 0x398) and every other mode entries 230–245. On other kinds it caps percent at 40 and blends stored colour sets into entries 10–82 (mode 0), 83–111 (mode 1) or 112–128 (other modes). It always ends with command 3. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x50cc seg5:0x5268 seg5:0x5814 DGROUP:0x178 DGROUP:0x17c DGROUP:0x180] Ordinal 14 with kind 1 and percent 40 copies DGROUP:0x1f2a into DGROUP:0x1e80. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x581c seg5:0x5836]
  - **Arithmetic.** For each byte v ordinal 14's kind-0 branch computes AX = v·p (imul), sign-extends AX with CWD (byte 0x99, which the listing prints as cdq), so the high word of the product is lost, divides by 100 with idiv and caps a quotient above 255 (signed) at 255 before it stores the low byte. So out = 255 when q > 255 and q & 0xff otherwise, with q = trunc(s16(v·p mod 65536)/100). For (3, 204), v ≤ 125 gives v·204/100, 126–160 give 255 and larger values wrap: 161 → 186, 180 → 224, 195 → 255 (q = −257), 200 → 9, 220 → 50, 240 → 91, 255 → 121. For (2, 137), v ≤ 186 gives v·137/100, 187–239 give 255, and 240 → 186, 250 → 200, 255 → 206. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x5181-0x5197 seg5:0x518c seg5:0x51cf-0x51e3 seg5:0x520c-0x5220 seg5:0x5228 seg5:0x5245 seg5:0x5262] [BYTES seg15:0x3604 seg15:0x3611]
  - INSPACE calls ordinal 14 in pairs: (3, p) then (2, (p + 200)/3) at seg15:0x299/0x2b0 and seg15:0x3e99/0x3eb0, (3, 49) then (2, 73) at seg15:0x1e93/0x1e9f, and (3, 204) then (2, 137) at seg15:0x3604/0x3611, where p = 100 · new level / previous level. Because the scaling is cumulative, these are stepwise fades of entries 185–229, with entries 230–245 moving a third as fast. [BYTES seg15:0x28c seg15:0x290 seg15:0x296 seg15:0x299 seg15:0x2a4 seg15:0x2b0 seg15:0x1e8f seg15:0x1e9b seg15:0x35ff seg15:0x360c seg15:0x3e90 seg15:0x3e99 seg15:0x3eb0 seg10:0x9948 seg10:0x994b]
- **Events.** Start-up seg6:0x190c creates the palette and loads scheme −1000 (all white, mode 2). Its caller seg6:0x18e0 then posts 0x9cf, whose handler deletes and recreates the palette, loads scheme 8 mode 2 and posts 0x9d0. The title frame seg15:0x5962 loads scheme 8 mode 1, and the state-1 initialiser seg10:0x0 loads scheme 8 mode 0 and animates. Exit deletes the palette (seg6:0x1a53). [BYTES seg6:0x18eb seg6:0x18fa seg6:0x1925 seg6:0x1941 seg2:0x458 seg2:0x469 seg2:0x484 seg2:0x4b6 seg15:0x5c35 seg10:0x37 seg10:0x3f seg6:0x1a53]
  - The save, blank, restore, grey-allocate and ordinal-14 calls sit in seg15:0x0, seg15:0xcb0, seg15:0x10be, seg15:0x1a42, seg15:0x302e, seg15:0x3590, seg15:0x3caa and seg16:0x2144 (with the state 6, 8 and 9 sequences seg16:0xcfe, seg16:0x6928, seg16:0x7210). The ramp allocations are in seg10:0x565a and seg15:0x6cdc. They are reached from the state 6, 8 and 9 ticks (seg7:0x18f, seg7:0x1b7, seg7:0x202) and also from the title frame (seg15:0x5985, seg15:0x59b3, seg15:0x5c89), seg11:0x7ab7, seg11:0x1674, seg11:0x1686 and seg9:0x6c6e. [BYTES seg10:0x56da seg15:0x6cfb seg7:0x18f seg7:0x1b7 seg7:0x202 seg15:0x5985 seg15:0x59b3 seg15:0x5c89 seg11:0x7ab7 seg11:0x1674 seg11:0x1686 seg9:0x6c6e]
- **Bitmaps.** ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x2080 builds bitmaps with CreateDIBitmap on GetDC(0) with the given palette selected and realised. For an 8-bit DIB it overwrites the colour table with the WORD indices 0–255 and passes DIB_PAL_COLORS; other DIBs use DIB_RGB_COLORS. Every 8-bit image pixel value is therefore an index into the game palette as it stands when the bitmap is created. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x20c2 seg5:0x20e4 seg5:0x20ef seg5:0x20fd seg5:0x211e seg5:0x2128 seg5:0x2148 seg5:0x215d seg5:0x215f]
  - A 4-bpp DIB such as ISPACE.DLL #4000 220, 221 or 300 goes through DIB_RGB_COLORS, so at true colour its device bitmap holds the DIB's own RGB values. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x215d seg5:0x215f] [REF Win16 SDK CreateDIBitmap]
  - At 8 bpp the same DIB_RGB_COLORS call has GDI match the table colours to the game palette realised in the DC when the bitmap is made. [REF Petzold, Programming Windows 3.1, palette manager: DIB_RGB_COLORS mapped to the selected logical palette] IGRAPHIC.DLL #4000 340, the credits logo, loads with d = 3 and a = b = 12, so seg5:0x26a2 writes no palette entry and applies no fixed set. Under the palette that the credits entry loads (scheme 8 in mode 0) the nearest entries by squared RGB distance, lowest index on ties, are 1 → 76 (dbdbdb), 2 → 9 (a6caf0), 3 → 104 (be96c8), 4 → 106 (a060ff), 5 → 107 (9050ff), 6 → 108 (8040ff), 7 → 20 (0000ff), 8 → 23 (0000bd), 9 → 4 (000080) and 15 → 0. Pixel indices 3–6 are 4091 of its 7523 non-black pixels, so at 8 bpp the logo is a purple and blue mix where a true-colour display shows blues. Matching against the system palette gives the same set, because the PC_RESERVED entries 10–245 hold their own system slots. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x2706 seg5:0x2bec seg5:0x3476 seg5:0x1bea seg5:0x2080 seg5:0x215f seg5:0x38bf] [BYTES seg15:0x7b54] [BYTES IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 340]
  - Graphics 220 and 221 take the same path: seg5:0x17c2 loads them through ordinal 1 with mode 3 and arg −1. Against scheme 8 alone (mode 2, the ramp at 103–112) 220's indices 0–10 map to 81, 81, 82, 83, 84, 85, 67, 86, 88, 89 and 72 (greys from #5500 100) and 221's to 73, 251, 30, 32, 32, 38, 43, 249, 52, 1 and 61. With all ramps (mode 0) 221's c4c400 maps to 125 and its 5a0000 to 172, so the palette current at the first strip build decides those two. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x17fd seg5:0x1805 seg5:0x180a seg5:0x1937 seg5:0x1941 seg5:0x1946 seg5:0x4713] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL 5500 100] [REF Petzold, Programming Windows 3.1, palette manager]
- **Graphic colour tables.** seg5:0x1b8e passes each loaded DIB to seg5:0x26a2 (cdecl, from seg5:0x1be2) with scheme bytes a and b and a last argument. When ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x15a ≠ 0 or the last argument ≠ 0, seg5:0x26a2 copies colour-table entries 0–9 into DGROUP:0x117c row 12 (DGROUP:0x12e4) and entries 10–14 into DGROUP:0x1cdc row 12 (DGROUP:0x1d90); a non-zero last argument also records a and b at DGROUP:0x1e80/0x1e82 + 6·slot. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x1be2 seg5:0x2810 seg5:0x2817 seg5:0x2839 seg5:0x286f seg5:0x289a]
  - It replaces the whole table with a fixed 16-colour set, and sets ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1ea8 = 1, exactly when entry 15's green (+0x65) or blue (+0x64) byte is non-zero, or DGROUP:0x188 ≠ 0 (set for ordinal 12) and its red byte (+0x66) is non-zero; seg5:0x280a then clears DGROUP:0x188 on both paths. A replacement makes ordinal 1 call seg6:0x7fc8 ([ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes)). [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x2706 seg5:0x270b seg5:0x2710 seg5:0x271a seg5:0x2721 seg5:0x272b seg5:0x280a seg5:0x571 seg5:0x57c] A 1-bit display (DGROUP:0x1e9a = 1) forces a = b = 0, and graphic id 0x118 skips the recolouring. Otherwise entries 0–9 become DGROUP:0x117c row a and entries 10–14 DGROUP:0x1cdc row b, each unless the byte is 12 or 0xff. Each entry 10–14 that then equals one of entries 0–9 is nudged by R + 4 (− 4 when R ≥ 0xfb) and G ± 4. At 8 bpp and above with d = 3 the pass then leaves through seg5:0x2bec → seg5:0x3476. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x270b seg5:0x272b seg5:0x28ad seg5:0x28b5 seg5:0x28c4 seg5:0x28db seg5:0x290d seg5:0x2924 seg5:0x2977 seg5:0x2a0c seg5:0x2bec]
  - For graphics 220, 221 and 300 loaded with a = b = 12, nothing applies (entry 15 is black and no entry 10–14 repeats one of 0–9), so their DIBs are used unchanged. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL 4000 220] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL 4000 221] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL 4000 300]
- **Title palette.** At 8 bpp the title's second picture copy (seg15:0x757c → seg10:0x73a6 → ordinal 12 → seg7:0x12d8 → seg5:0x1b8e in mode 0 → seg5:0x26a2 with a = 1, b = 12) writes palette entries 185–229 as gold and grey ramps from graphic 300's colours; seg5:0x1b8e then animates. No command 6 runs on the title path (seg10:0x6d7a has only the callers seg15:0x363d, seg15:0x367b and seg16:0x21bf), and the first picture copy (ordinal 1, mode 3) writes no palette entry. The complete title palette is in [palette-tables.md](palette-tables.md#title-palette-at-8-bpp). [BYTES seg15:0x757c seg10:0x73a6 seg15:0x5c35 seg15:0x6cfb seg15:0x7394 seg10:0x6d7a] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg6:0xc4 seg7:0x137f seg7:0x138d seg5:0x2e87 seg5:0x2eb4 seg5:0x310c seg5:0x3200 seg5:0x33ab seg5:0x1bff] [ORACLE probe-0001] [ORACLE wave-0001]
- **Colours.** ISPACE.DLL seg8:0x27f0, the colour helper of the ordinal-32 renderer (17 call sites), returns PALETTEINDEX(n) (DX = 0x0100) when max(BITSPIXEL, PLANES) is exactly 8 and a PALETTERGB colour (high byte 2) otherwise. INSPACE passes immediate colours as RGB (high byte 0) or PALETTERGB (high byte 2); no immediate PALETTEINDEX was found. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg8:0x280d seg8:0x2874 seg8:0x28cf] [BYTES seg7:0x1fb3 seg11:0x115 seg15:0x7200]
- **Second palette.** ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x26a2 builds a separate 16-entry palette with CreatePalette at seg5:0x2c68 and keeps it in ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1eae. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg5:0x2c5a seg5:0x2c68 seg5:0x2c6d]
- **UpdateColors.** INSPACE imports UpdateColors (GDI.366) once, at seg2:0x5a2, on the palette-message path next to SelectPalette/RealizePalette with DGROUP:0x920e. A true-colour port has no equivalent for it. [BYTES seg2:0x589 seg2:0x59a seg2:0x5a2]

## Help
INSPACE has two help paths: WinHelp for the manual and the IS_HELP Online Help window (state 7).

- **WinHelp.** seg2:0x190a(DWORD ctx) calls WinHelp(DGROUP:0x16, Path + Language + "_HELP.HLP", HELP_INDEX (3) when ctx is 0 and HELP_CONTEXT (1) otherwise, ctx). Path comes from DGROUP:0x8f7a. Language is read from INSPACE.INI (default "") with nSize 5, so at most 4 characters, into the shared string buffer DGROUP:0x8e68, which seg2:0x94c also fills with strings of up to 249 bytes. It is INSPACE.EXE's only WinHelp call, so the game never sends HELP_QUIT. [BYTES seg2:0x1918 seg2:0x1927 seg2:0x1934 seg2:0x194c seg2:0x1952 seg2:0x1961 seg2:0x196d seg2:0x1987 seg2:0x961]
- seg2:0x190a has two call sites: seg2:0x156b in the WM_COMMAND handler and seg6:0x2379 in the Navigation window. In both help files' context maps id 5 is General and id 28 is Credits, and HELP_INDEX opens the contents topic Contents. [BYTES seg2:0x156b seg2:0x1574 seg2:0x1576 seg2:0x1566 seg2:0x157a seg2:0x157c seg2:0x1633 seg2:0x1638 seg2:0x163d seg2:0x164b seg2:0x1552 seg2:0x1557 seg2:0x1652 seg6:0x22fc seg6:0x2375 seg6:0x2377 seg6:0x2379 seg6:0x2482 seg6:0x24b0 seg6:0x24ba] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 135] id 2144 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL MENU 101] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL ACCELERATOR MAINACCEL] [MANUAL General] [MANUAL Credits] [MANUAL Contents]

  | Source | Command | ctx | Topic opened |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | About Inner Space | 0xc9 | 0x1c | Credits |
  | User Manual | 0xca | 0 | Contents |
  | Playing Inner Space | 0xcb | 5 | General |
  | F1 while DGROUP:0xa7c is 0 | 0xf0 | 0 | Contents |
  | F2 | 0x100 | 0 | Contents |
  | Navigation button 0x70 "&Help" (string 2144) | none | 5 | General |

- The ENG_HELP.HLP that v1.29.08 opens is the 2000 file: INSTALL.INI lists ENG_HELP.HLP in its group 1 for the game directory, and the ship disk carries that file as Eng_help.hl1. Reading destination 0 as the game directory relies on oracle/install_layout.py. [BYTES INSTALL.INI] [BYTES Eng_help.hl1]
- **Online Help.** seg2:0x88e opens the IS_HELP window only while DGROUP:0xa7c ≠ 0 (the IS_INFO targeting display) and DGROUP:0x9460 = 0, so Online Help opens only while the HUD exists. It uses class IS_HELP, title string 2182, style 0x10c80000, position HelpLeft, HelpTop (DGROUP:0x1dc4, DGROUP:0x1dc6), width DGROUP:0x90ea − 80 clamped to 640–760, height SM_CYCAPTION + 345 and the main window as owner, then HWND_TOPMOST. Opening and closing the window sets states 7 and 1 ([globals.md](globals.md#game-state-globals)). [BYTES seg2:0x89b seg2:0x8a2 seg2:0x8a9 seg2:0x8ac seg2:0x8b2 seg2:0x8ba seg2:0x8cc seg2:0x8d7 seg2:0x8e9 seg2:0x8f9 seg2:0x901 seg2:0x906 seg2:0x90a seg2:0x913 seg2:0x91c seg2:0x92a seg2:0x93f] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 137] id 2182 [ORACLE wave-0001]
- **Topic.** The help topic DGROUP:0x93c6 is written only at seg2:0x1559 (4), seg2:0x1580 (5), seg2:0x1588 (6), seg2:0x1590 (1), seg2:0x1598 (0), seg2:0x10b2 (6), seg2:0x10fa (2), seg2:0x1149 (5), seg11:0x236e (1) and seg11:0x2376 (0). Its seg17 readers also compare it with 3, a value no writer stores. [BYTES seg2:0x1559 seg2:0x1580 seg2:0x1588 seg2:0x1590 seg2:0x1598 seg2:0x10b2 seg2:0x10fa seg2:0x1149 seg11:0x236e seg11:0x2376 seg17:0x7383]
- Outside the wave, where DGROUP:0xa7c is 0, the Help menu's General, Scores, This Game, Target and Weapons items do nothing and F1 opens WinHelp at Contents. Inside the wave F1 opens IS_HELP with topic 4. [BYTES seg2:0x89b seg2:0x8a0 seg2:0x1552 seg2:0x1557 seg2:0x1559 seg2:0x1560]
- **Closing the HUD.** seg11:0x2f0 destroys and zeroes DGROUP:0xa7a, DGROUP:0xa7c and DGROUP:0xa7e. Its callers are seg6:0x5da4 (state-5 entry), seg9:0xd81 (seg9:0xd6c, both the state-4 and the abort paths), seg9:0x6d5c (state-2 entry), seg10:0x259 (wave teardown), seg11:0x2e5, seg16:0xd96 (state-6 phase 0), seg16:0x697a (state-8 phase 0), seg16:0x7223 (state-9 phase 0) and seg18:0x3aa. [BYTES seg2:0x89b-0x8a9 seg11:0x2fa-0x336]

## Open questions
- What first runs seg2:0xa06 after start-up. DGROUP:0x8f68 is BSS, so the loop waits in GetMessage and runs no idle frame until the flag becomes 1. At start-up the only reachable setter is seg2:0xa06 (seg2:0xa66), which runs on WM_SETFOCUS while the window is not iconic and the flag is 0, or from the WM_TIMER resume branch. SC_RESTORE (seg2:0xea6) and the child windows (seg7:0x6298, seg13:0x4f9f, seg13:0x6250) also set it. Whether the WM_SETFOCUS from ShowWindow(SW_SHOW) at seg15:0x5840, or the 0x914 ShowWindow/SetActiveWindow at seg2:0x7df after the first WM_TIMER, first runs seg2:0xa06 needs an oracle trace. [OPEN] [BYTES seg2:0xc9 seg2:0xe3 seg2:0x7f6 seg2:0x80d seg2:0x132c seg2:0x1341 seg2:0xa66 seg2:0xea6 seg15:0x5840 seg2:0x7df]
- The runtime content of the low-resource MessageBox text and caption, which follow uninitialised stack bytes. [OPEN] [BYTES seg6:0x141e seg6:0x1424 seg6:0x1431 seg6:0x1437 seg10:0xa37b seg10:0xa385 seg10:0xa3b6]
- Whether CTL3D loads with the shipped installer: INSPACE looks only in the Windows directory, while INSTALL.INI puts CTL3D.DLL in destination 8, which oracle/install_layout.py infers as the system directory. [OPEN] [BYTES seg2:0x19b2 seg2:0x19bd seg2:0x19d3] [BYTES INSTALL.INI]
- What the game does after a cancelled Alt-X Save & Exit, which returns with DGROUP:0x1a still 1 (writers that clear it: seg7:0x441, seg10:0x2e, seg11:0x7b5f, seg15:0x57fc). [OPEN] [BYTES seg2:0xe07 seg10:0x862d seg10:0x8636]
- Roles of DGROUP:0x94e4, DGROUP:0x94fa and DGROUP:0x967a, filled from strings 2900–2902 at start-up. seg11:0xb306 looks the key up in the DGROUP:0x94fa table and returns DGROUP:0x94e4. [OPEN] [BYTES seg6:0x1789 seg6:0x17bd seg11:0xb313 seg11:0xb32d seg11:0xb337 seg3:0x1577]
- The registration-code generator ISPACE.DLL seg10:0x9b6 with its inputs ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x154c and DGROUP:0x1550, and the serial writer ISPACE.DLL seg10:0x5dfe (IGRAPHIC.DLL #5500 110). [OPEN] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg10:0x9b6 seg10:0x87b seg10:0x5dfe]
- ENG_LANG strings 2122 "Registered copy" and 2123 "Unregistered copy": no immediate operand in INSPACE.EXE or ISPACE.DLL names them, so their user is unknown. [OPEN] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 133] ids 2122–2123
- What the 0x50-byte table at DGROUP:0x2f8a gates while DGROUP:0x11aa = 0 (Plus-only content). [OPEN] [BYTES seg14:0x33a seg14:0xfb7 seg14:0x16e5 seg14:0x4567 seg16:0x3d66 seg16:0x4c1a seg16:0x4c51 seg16:0x5169]

- The exact per-pixel rule of ordinal 12's smoothed 2× render of graphic 300 (ISPACE.DLL seg7:0x12d8–0x4ee5, ramp builder seg7:0x4ee8, plane buffers ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0xda8 and DGROUP:0xec8, helpers seg5:0x22e6 and seg5:0x24be). The best simple model, level(2y + oy, 2x + ox) = ceil((2L(y, x) + L(y + dy, x) + L(y, x + dx))/4) on the ramp levels (gold 3(i − 1), grey 4(i − 10); dy, dx = −1 for phase 0 and +1 for phase 1), matches 76 % of interior pixels of the probe's emblem, and the rest are one level off. A pixel-exact title emblem needs the rule. [OPEN] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg7:0x12d8 seg7:0x4ee8 seg7:0x139d] [ORACLE probe-0001]

- The meal window and the oracle. DGROUP:0x9214 comes from seg10:0x9672, called by seg15:0xf2e at desk entry and by seg9:0x2af6. With t = hour × 100 + minute + 1 it tests INI Lunch (1200) → 3, Breakfast (800) → 4, Coffee (1000) → 1 and Tea (1530) → 2 in that order and takes the first key K with K < t < K + 30; otherwise 0. Its writers are seg10:0x96c7, seg10:0x96f4, seg10:0x9720, seg10:0x974c, seg10:0x9754 and seg15:0xf3b. So "GOOD LUCK" against "ENJOY YOUR FOOD" depends on the host clock, and a LAUNCH line ending in FOOD needs the breakfast window (08:00–08:28 by default). Whether the oracle's FOOD run fell in that window is not established. [OPEN] [BYTES seg10:0x9698 seg10:0x96b2 seg10:0x96c7 seg10:0x96df seg10:0x96f4 seg10:0x970b seg10:0x9720 seg10:0x9737 seg10:0x974c seg10:0x9754 seg15:0xf3b seg15:0xf41 seg9:0x2af6]
